I… I came in because I can’t seem to get past this feeling—no, it’s more than a feeling, it feels like I know it—that I’m being punished for what happened with my father. For letting him decline. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… yes, I do. I know people keep telling me that it wasn’t all on me, that my father’s health was complicated and that I couldn’t control everything, but I still go back over every single decision like I’m building a case against myself.

Like, I think about his appointments—whether I scheduled them quickly enough, whether I asked the right questions, whether I should have pushed harder or noticed something sooner. And then I think about conversations with my relatives, especially the tense ones, and I replay every word I said. If I got defensive, or if I sounded tired, or if I didn’t answer someone right away, it turns into proof in my head that I’m selfish. Like I was more concerned with my own stress than with being a good daughter.

And it doesn’t stay only with my father, either. It spreads. I’ll be at work and make some small mistake or take too long to respond to something, and suddenly it feels like I’m not just struggling, I’m useless. A useless employee, a selfish daughter… those are the words that keep coming up. And then there’s this awful part of me that thinks, well, maybe whatever happens to me now is deserved. Like I don’t really get to complain because I brought it on myself somehow.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n2\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.3, F43.12, F33.3, F22, F06.2, F66.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I… I came in because I can’t seem to get past this feeling—no, it’s more than a feeling, it feels like I know it—that I’m being punished for what happened with my father. For letting him decline. I keep going over it in my head, all the decisions, all the appointments, the things I did and didn’t notice, and it feels like there’s already been some kind of judgment made about me.\n\nMy sister keeps trying to show me the doctor’s notes, and she tells me I did everything I was supposed to do, that I didn’t cause it, that no one thinks that. And I can understand the words when she says them, but they don’t… they don’t land. It still feels certain, like I failed him and now this is what I deserve somehow. I know that probably sounds extreme, but I can’t shake it, and it’s been sitting on me constantly.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about this feeling of being punished—do you find that you blame yourself for things that weren’t really your fault, or do you feel guilty even when others say you did nothing wrong?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do. I know people keep telling me that it wasn’t all on me, that my father’s health was complicated and that I couldn’t control everything, but I still go back over every single decision like I’m building a case against myself.\n\nLike, I think about his appointments—whether I scheduled them quickly enough, whether I asked the right questions, whether I should have pushed harder or noticed something sooner. And then I think about conversations with my relatives, especially the tense ones, and I replay every word I said. Yes… most days, yes. Even when nothing is actively going wrong, it’s like my mind finds a way back to it. I keep going over every decision I made about my father’s appointments—whether I called soon enough, whether I should’ve asked more, whether I missed something obvious—and then it turns into this conclusion that I failed him.

with.\n\n[Symptom Profiles]\n- key: S019\n  name: Delusions\n  description: Fixed beliefs that do not change easily, even in the face of contradictory evidence.\n  manifestation: I came in because I judgment has already been passed on me.\n- key: S020\n  name: Hallucinations\n  description: Perception-like experiences that occur without an external stimulus and are as vivid and clear as normal perception.\n  manifestation: When the house is quiet at night, I sometimes hear a woman's voice as clearly as if she's in the hallway saying, 'You failed him,' and I've gotten up more than once to hollow feeling, like the color has gone out of everything, and I can sit at my desk in HR or stare at the garden beds and feel like none of it reaches me anymore.\n- key: S027\n  name: Sleep_Disturbance\n  description: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.\n  manifestation: I fall asleep late after replaying phone calls and care decisions, then I'm awake around 3 or 4 in the morning with my chest tight, unable to get back to sleep before work.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n  description: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.\n  manifestation: People at work have started asking if I'm sick because I move so slowly through routine tasks, and sometimes it takes me a noticeable moment to answer even or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep going over every decision about my father's appointments and every tense conversation with my relatives, and I end up feeling like I'm a selfish daughter and a useless employee who deserves whatever happens.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I don't have a plan to hurt myself, but I catch myself thinking it would be easier if I didn't wake up, especially on mornings when I know there are more calls from the care agency and problems waiting at work.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the major depressive episode\n  manifestation: I never heard voices or believed I was being punished before this depression settled in around my father's decline; those experiences only started after months of feeling low, guilty, and worn down.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I've missed deadlines, avoided difficult employee meetings I normally handle, and stopped showing up for library volunteering because I can't keep myself steady enough to follow through.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about a year and two months, since my father's health and behavior started changing and I had to step into caregiving.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 46 / human_resources_worker / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I… I came in because I can’t seem to get past this feeling—no, it’s more than a feeling, it feels like I know it—that I’m being punished for what happened with my father. For letting him decline. I keep going over it in my head, all the decisions, all the appointments, the things I did and didn’t notice, and it feels like there’s already been some kind of judgment made about me.\n\nMy sister keeps trying to show me the doctor’s notes, and she tells me I did everything I was supposed to do, that I didn’t cause it, that no one thinks that. And I can understand the words when she says them, but they don’t… they don’t land. It still feels certain, like I failed him and now this is what I deserve somehow. I know that probably sounds extreme, but I can’t shake it, and it’s been sitting on me constantly.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about this feeling of being punished—do you find that you blame yourself for things that weren’t really your fault, or do you feel guilty even when others say you did nothing wrong?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do. I know people keep telling me that it wasn’t all on me, that my father’s health was complicated and that I couldn’t control everything, but I still go back over every single decision like I’m building a case against myself.\n\nLike, I think about his appointments—whether I scheduled them quickly enough, whether I asked the right questions, whether I should have pushed harder or noticed something sooner. And then I think about conversations with my relatives, especially the tense ones, and I replay every word I said. If I got defensive, or if I sounded tired, or if I didn’t answer someone right away, it turns into proof in my head that I’m selfish. Like I was more concerned with my own stress than with being a good daughter.\n\nAnd it doesn’t stay only with my father, either. It spreads. I’ll be at work and make some small mistake or take too long to respond to something, and suddenly it feels like I’m not just struggling, I’m useless. A useless employee, a selfish daughter… those are the words that keep coming up. And then there’s this awful part of me that thinks, well, maybe whatever happens to me now is deserved. Like I don’t really get to complain because I brought it on myself somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this sense of worthlessness or that you are a bad or selfish person most days, even when things are going okay?\n\nPatient: Yes… most days, yes. Even when nothing is actively going wrong, it’s like my mind finds a way back to it. I keep going over every decision I made about my father’s appointments—whether I called soon enough, whether I should’ve asked more, whether I missed something obvious—and then it turns into this conclusion that I failed him.\n\nAnd it’s not just that. I replay those tense conversations with relatives, too. If I was short with someone, or if I seemed frustrated, I end up thinking, see, that’s who you are, you’re selfish, you made it about yourself. It gets very… fixed in my head.\n\nThen at work, if I make even a small mistake or don’t handle something perfectly, I start thinking I’m useless there too. Like I’m a selfish daughter and a useless employee, and whatever happens to me after that is something I’ve earned somehow. Even when things are technically okay, I don’t really feel okay inside.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you found yourself losing interest in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies, work, or spending time with friends or family?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I’m… I’m not sure how to answer that clearly. I don’t know if I’d say I’ve noticed it in a clean way, like “I used to love this and now I don’t.” It’s more muddled than that.

I still do some things, or at least I think about doing them, but I can’t always tell whether I’m not interested or whether I’m just too caught up in everything else in my head. Like with friends or family, I sometimes don’t know if I’m avoiding people because I don’t enjoy being around them, or because I feel uncomfortable and don’t know how to act normal. So I guess I haven’t sat down and thought, “I’ve lost interest in all my hobbies.” I don’t even know what counts right now. It’s just that everything feels harder to sort out than it used to.

library volunteering because I can't keep myself steady enough to follow through.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about a year and two months, since my father's health and behavior started changing and I had to step into caregiving.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 46 / human_resources_worker / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about this feeling of being punished—do you find that you blame yourself for things that weren’t really your fault, or do you feel guilty even when others say you did nothing wrong?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do. I know people keep telling me that it wasn’t all on me, that my father’s health was complicated and that I couldn’t control everything, but I still go back over every single decision like I’m building a case against myself.\n\nLike, I think about his appointments—whether I scheduled them quickly enough, whether I asked the right questions, whether I should have pushed harder or noticed something sooner. And then I think about conversations with my relatives, especially the tense ones, and I replay every word I said. If I got defensive, or if I sounded tired, or if I didn’t answer someone right away, it turns into proof in my head that I’m selfish. Like I was more concerned with my own stress than with being a good daughter.\n\nAnd it doesn’t stay only with my father, either. It spreads. I’ll be at work and make some small mistake or take too long to respond to something, and suddenly it feels like I’m not just struggling, I’m useless. A useless employee, a selfish daughter… those are the words that keep coming up. And then there’s this awful part of me that thinks, well, maybe whatever happens to me now is deserved. Like I don’t really get to complain because I brought it on myself somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this sense of worthlessness or that you are a bad or selfish person most days, even when things are going okay?\n\nPatient: Yes… most days, yes. Even when nothing is actively going wrong, it’s like my mind finds a way back to it. I keep going over every decision I made about my father’s appointments—whether I called soon enough, whether I should’ve asked more, whether I missed something obvious—and then it turns into this conclusion that I failed him.\n\nAnd it’s not just that. I replay those tense conversations with relatives, too. If I was short with someone, or if I seemed frustrated, I end up thinking, see, that’s who you are, you’re selfish, you made it about yourself. It gets very… fixed in my head.\n\nThen at work, if I make even a small mistake or don’t handle something perfectly, I start thinking I’m useless there too. Like I’m a selfish daughter and a useless employee, and whatever happens to me after that is something I’ve earned somehow. Even when things are technically okay, I don’t really feel okay inside.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself losing interest in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies, work, or spending time with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not sure how to answer that clearly. I don’t know if I’d say I’ve noticed it in a clean way, like “I used to love this and now I don’t.” It’s more muddled than that.\n\nI still do some things, or at least I think about doing them, but I can’t always tell whether I’m not interested or whether I’m just too caught up in everything else in my head. Like with friends or family, I sometimes don’t know if I’m avoiding people because I don’t enjoy being around them, or because I feel uncomfortable and don’t know how to act normal. And work is… work. I go through it, but I wouldn’t really describe it as enjoyment exactly.\n\nSo I guess I haven’t sat down and thought, “I’ve lost interest in all my hobbies.” I don’t even know what counts right now. It’s just that everything feels harder to sort out than it used to.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you felt down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for the past couple of weeks?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think so. Most days, when I wake up, there’s this heavy feeling already there, before anything has even happened. It’s hard to describe without sounding dramatic, but it feels hollow, like the color has gone out of everything.

I’ll get to work and sit at my desk in HR, looking at emails or forms or whatever is in front of me, and it’s like I’m there physically but nothing really reaches me. Or I’ll look out at the garden beds at home—something I used to at least notice, the plants, the little changes—and I just feel… flat. Not peaceful, not rested, just kind of emptied out.

It’s not every single second, I suppose, but it’s there most of the day, most days. Like a weight I’m carrying around even when I’m trying to act normal.

bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this sense of worthlessness or that you are a bad or selfish person most days, even when things are going okay?\n\nPatient: Yes… most days, yes. Even when nothing is actively going wrong, it’s like my mind finds a way back to it. I keep going over every decision I made about my father’s appointments—whether I called soon enough, whether I should’ve asked more, whether I missed something obvious—and then it turns into this conclusion that I failed him.\n\nAnd it’s not just that. I replay those tense conversations with relatives, too. If I was short with someone, or if I seemed frustrated, I end up thinking, see, that’s who you are, you’re selfish, you made it about yourself. It gets very… fixed in my head.\n\nThen at work, if I make even a small mistake or don’t handle something perfectly, I start thinking I’m useless there too. Like I’m a selfish daughter and a useless employee, and whatever happens to me after that is something I’ve earned somehow. Even when things are technically okay, I don’t really feel okay inside.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself losing interest in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies, work, or spending time with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not sure how to answer that clearly. I don’t know if I’d say I’ve noticed it in a clean way, like “I used to love this and now I don’t.” It’s more muddled than that.\n\nI still do some things, or at least I think about doing them, but I can’t always tell whether I’m not interested or whether I’m just too caught up in everything else in my head. Like with friends or family, I sometimes don’t know if I’m avoiding people because I don’t enjoy being around them, or because I feel uncomfortable and don’t know how to act normal. And work is… work. I go through it, but I wouldn’t really describe it as enjoyment exactly.\n\nSo I guess I haven’t sat down and thought, “I’ve lost interest in all my hobbies.” I don’t even know what counts right now. It’s just that everything feels harder to sort out than it used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for the past couple of weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. Most days, when I wake up, there’s this heavy feeling already there, before anything has even happened. It’s hard to describe without sounding dramatic, but it feels hollow, like the color has gone out of everything.\n\nI’ll get to work and sit at my desk in HR, looking at emails or forms or whatever is in front of me, and it’s like I’m there physically but nothing really reaches me. Or I’ll look out at the garden beds at home—something I used to at least notice, the plants, the little changes—and I just feel… flat. Not peaceful, not rested, just kind of emptied out.\n\nIt’s not every single second, I suppose, but it’s there most of the day, most days. Like a weight I’m carrying around even when I’m trying to act normal.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you noticed a decrease in your energy level or felt more fatigued than usual, even when you haven’t been doing much physically?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I don’t really know. I mean, I feel tired a lot, but I’m not sure if it’s a separate thing or just… everything else wearing me down.

about a year and two months, since my father's health and behavior started changing and I had to step into caregiving.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 46 / human_resources_worker / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you found yourself losing interest in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies, work, or spending time with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not sure how to answer that clearly. I don’t know if I’d say I’ve noticed it in a clean way, like “I used to love this and now I don’t.” It’s more muddled than that.\n\nI still do some things, or at least I think about doing them, but I can’t always tell whether I’m not interested or whether I’m just too caught up in everything else in my head. Most days, when I wake up, there’s this heavy feeling already there, before anything has even happened. Like a weight I’m carrying around even when I’m trying to act normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your energy level or felt more fatigued than usual, even when you haven’t been doing much physically?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking my weight or anything, so I don’t want to say I’ve lost or gained if I don’t actually know. My clothes seem… I don’t know, about the same, I think.

So… no, I don’t think I’ve noticed a clear appetite or weight change. It’s more that I’m not very tuned in to it.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes. Sleep has definitely been… off. I don’t usually fall asleep when I mean to anymore. I’ll get into bed and I’m exhausted, but then my mind starts going over phone calls from the day, or decisions I made about someone’s care, or whether I said the wrong thing or missed something, and it just loops. I’ll replay the same conversation in my head like I can somehow fix it by thinking about it harder.

So I end up falling asleep late, later than I should, even when I’m lying there trying not to think. And then I’ll wake up around 3 or 4 in the morning, almost like my body has decided that’s it, sleep is over. My chest feels tight when I wake up, and I’ll lie there staring at the dark, trying to get comfortable or calm down, but I usually can’t really get back to sleep properly before I have to get up for work.

It’s frustrating because by the time morning comes, I feel like I never really had a full night. I’m not staying up doing anything useful, either. It’s just… lying there, thinking and waking too early.

For letting him decline. I keep going over it in my head, all the decisions, all the appointments, the things I did and didn’t notice, and it feels like there’s already been some kind of judgment made about me.\n\nMy sister keeps trying to show me the doctor’s notes, and she tells me I did everything I was supposed to do, that I didn’t cause it, that no one thinks that. And I can understand the words when she says them, but they don’t… they don’t land. It still feels certain, like I failed him and now this is what I deserve somehow. I know that probably sounds extreme, but I can’t shake it, and it’s been sitting on me constantly.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about this feeling of being punished—do you find that you blame yourself for things that weren’t really your fault, or do you feel guilty even when others say you did nothing wrong?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do. I know people keep telling me that it wasn’t all on me, that my father’s health was complicated and that I couldn’t control everything, but I still go back over every single decision like I’m building a case against myself.\n\nLike, I think about his appointments—whether I scheduled them quickly enough, whether I asked the right questions, whether I should have pushed harder or noticed something sooner. And then I think about conversations with my relatives, especially the tense ones, and I replay every word I said. If I got defensive, or if I sounded tired, or if I didn’t answer someone right away, it turns into proof in my head that I’m selfish. Like I was more concerned with my own stress than with being a good daughter.\n\nAnd it doesn’t stay only with my father, either. It spreads. I’ll be at work and make some small mistake or take too long to respond to something, and suddenly it feels like I’m not just struggling, I’m useless. A useless employee, a selfish daughter… those are the words that keep coming up. And then there’s this awful part of me that thinks, well, maybe whatever happens to me now is deserved. Like I don’t really get to complain because I brought it on myself somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this sense of worthlessness or that you are a bad or selfish person most days, even when things are going okay?\n\nPatient: Yes… most days, yes. Even when nothing is actively going wrong, it’s like my mind finds a way back to it. I keep going over every decision I made about my father’s appointments—whether I called soon enough, whether I should’ve asked more, whether I missed something obvious—and then it turns into this conclusion that I failed him.\n\nAnd it’s not just that. I replay those tense conversations with relatives, too. If I was short with someone, or if I seemed frustrated, I end up thinking, see, that’s who you are, you’re selfish, you made it about yourself. It gets very… fixed in my head.\n\nThen at work, if I make even a small mistake or don’t handle something perfectly, I start thinking I’m useless there too. Like I was more concerned with my own stress than with being a good daughter.\n\nAnd it doesn’t stay only with my father, either. It spreads. I’ll be at work and make some small mistake or take too long to respond to something, and suddenly it feels like I’m not just struggling, I’m useless. A useless employee, a selfish daughter… those are the words that keep coming up. And then there’s this awful part of me that thinks, well, maybe whatever happens to me now is deserved. Like I don’t really get to complain because I brought it on myself somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this sense of worthlessness or that you are a bad or selfish person most days, even when things are going okay?\n\nPatient: Yes… most days, yes. Even when nothing is actively going wrong, it’s like my mind finds a way back to it. I keep going over every decision I made about my father’s appointments—whether I called soon enough, whether I should’ve asked more, whether I missed something obvious—and then it turns into this conclusion that I failed him.\n\nAnd it’s not just that. I replay those tense conversations with relatives, too. If I was short with someone, or if I seemed frustrated, I end up thinking, see, that’s who you are, you’re selfish, you made it about yourself. It gets very… fixed in my head.\n\nThen at work, if I make even a small mistake or don’t handle something perfectly, I start thinking I’m useless there too. Like I’m a selfish daughter and a useless employee, and whatever happens to me after that is something I’ve earned somehow. Even when things are technically okay, I don’t really feel okay inside.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself losing interest in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies, work, or spending time with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not sure how to answer that clearly. I don’t know if I’d say I’ve noticed it in a clean way, like “I used to love this and now I don’t.” It’s more muddled than that.\n\nI still do some things, or at least I think about doing them, but I can’t always tell whether I’m not interested or whether I’m just too caught up in everything else in my head. Like with friends or family, I sometimes don’t know if I’m avoiding people because I don’t enjoy being around them, or because I feel uncomfortable and don’t know how to act normal. And work is… work. I go through it, but I wouldn’t really describe it as enjoyment exactly.\n\nSo I guess I haven’t sat down and thought, “I’ve lost interest in all my hobbies.” I don’t even know what counts right now. It’s just that everything feels harder to sort out than it used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for the past couple of weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. Most days, when I wake up, there’s this heavy feeling already there, before anything has even happened. It’s hard to describe without sounding dramatic, but it feels hollow, like the color has gone out of everything.\n\nI’ll get to work and sit at my desk in HR, looking at emails or forms or whatever is in front of me, and it’s like I’m there physically but nothing really reaches me. Or I’ll look out at the garden beds at home—something I used to at least notice, the plants, the little changes—and I just feel… flat. Not peaceful, not rested, just kind of emptied out.\n\nIt’s not every single second, I suppose, but it’s there most of the day, most days. Like a weight I’m carrying around even when I’m trying to act normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your energy level or felt more fatigued than usual, even when you haven’t been doing much physically?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I feel tired a lot, but I’m not sure if it’s a separate thing or just… everything else wearing me down.\n\nSome days I come home from work and I feel like I’ve done much more than I actually did, if that makes sense. Like even sitting through normal tasks or answering emails can feel like it took a lot out of me. But then I wonder if that counts as fatigue, or if I’m just mentally drained.\n\nI haven’t been doing anything especially physical, no. I’m not out exercising or lifting things or anything like that. It’s more like I don’t feel refreshed very often, but I don’t know how to measure it. I wouldn’t want to say it wrong. I’ve noticed I feel worn down, but I’m not sure whether my actual energy level has changed or whether I’m just noticing it more because everything feels harder.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently—like eating more or less than usual, or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking my weight or anything, so I don’t want to say I’ve lost or gained if I don’t actually know. My clothes seem… I don’t know, about the same, I think.\n\nWith eating, it’s a little hard for me to answer clearly. Some days I eat because it’s time to eat, not because I particularly want anything. Other times I’ll realize I haven’t paid much attention to whether I’m hungry or not. But I can’t say there’s been some obvious big change, like suddenly eating twice as much or barely eating at all.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve noticed a clear appetite or weight change. It’s more that I’m not very tuned in to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep recently—like having trouble falling asleep, waking up during the night, or waking up earlier than you want to?\n\nPatient: Yes. Sleep has definitely been… off. I don’t usually fall asleep when I mean to anymore. I’ll get into bed and I’m exhausted, but then my mind starts going over phone calls from the day, or decisions I made about someone’s care, or whether I said the wrong thing or missed something, and it just loops. I’ll replay the same conversation in my head like I can somehow fix it by thinking about it harder.\n\nSo I end up falling asleep late, later than I should, even when I’m lying there trying not to think. And then I’ll wake up around 3 or 4 in the morning, almost like my body has decided that’s it, sleep is over. My chest feels tight when I wake up, and I’ll lie there staring at the dark, trying to get comfortable or calm down, but I usually can’t really get back to sleep properly before I have to get up for work.\n\nIt’s frustrating because by the time morning comes, I feel like I never really had a full night. I’m not staying up doing anything useful, either. It’s just… lying there, thinking and waking too early.\n\nDoctor: Have you had episodes like this before—where you felt down, guilty, and lost interest in things—or is this the first time you’ve experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve had times before where I was stressed or unhappy, like anyone has, but I don’t know if it was the same as this. I haven’t really thought of my life in terms of “episodes,” if that makes sense.\n\nThere have been periods where I was more withdrawn or harder on myself, but I can’t confidently say, yes, this exact thing happened before. I don’t want to exaggerate or make it sound like there’s a clear pattern if I’m not sure there is one. This feels… noticeable to me now, but whether it’s the first time or just the first time I’m paying this much attention to it, I honestly don’t know.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’m trying to think of it in the way you’re asking—like a distinct block of time, two weeks or more, where I could say, yes, I was clearly down or hopeless or not interested in things. And honestly, I don’t know that I can identify one clearly.

I’ve had rough stretches before, sure. There were times I was overwhelmed or not really myself, times I probably pulled back from people or went through the motions more than anything. But when I look back, it all kind of blends into life stress, work stress, being tired, dealing with responsibilities… I don’t have a clean memory of, “this started here and lasted until there,” if that makes sense.

Part of me worries I’m just not remembering it right. I tend to downplay things after the fact, or tell myself it wasn’t that bad. But I also don’t want to make something sound more definite than it is. So the most honest answer is: I can’t really recall a distinct past period like that with certainty. I know I’ve had difficult times, but I can’t say confidently that there was a clear two-week episode before.

It’s more like I don’t feel refreshed very often, but I don’t know how to measure it. I wouldn’t want to say it wrong. I’ve noticed I feel worn down, but I’m not sure whether my actual energy level has changed or whether I’m just noticing it more because everything feels harder.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently—like eating more or less than usual, or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking my weight or anything, so I don’t want to say I’ve lost or gained if I don’t actually know. My clothes seem… I don’t know, about the same, I think.\n\nWith eating, it’s a little hard for me to answer clearly. Some days I eat because it’s time to eat, not because I particularly want anything. Other times I’ll realize I haven’t paid much attention to whether I’m hungry or not. But I can’t say there’s been some obvious big change, like suddenly eating twice as much or barely eating at all.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve noticed a clear appetite or weight change. It’s more that I’m not very tuned in to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep recently—like having trouble falling asleep, waking up during the night, or waking up earlier than you want to?\n\nPatient: Yes. Sleep has definitely been… off. I don’t usually fall asleep when I mean to anymore. I’ll get into bed and I’m exhausted, but then my mind starts going over phone calls from the day, or decisions I made about someone’s care, or whether I said the wrong thing or missed something, and it just loops. I’ll replay the same conversation in my head like I can somehow fix it by thinking about it harder.\n\nSo I end up falling asleep late, later than I should, even when I’m lying there trying not to think. And then I’ll wake up around 3 or 4 in the morning, almost like my body has decided that’s it, sleep is over. My chest feels tight when I wake up, and I’ll lie there staring at the dark, trying to get comfortable or calm down, but I usually can’t really get back to sleep properly before I have to get up for work.\n\nIt’s frustrating because by the time morning comes, I feel like I never really had a full night. I’m not staying up doing anything useful, either. It’s just… lying there, thinking and waking too early.\n\nDoctor: Have you had episodes like this before—where you felt down, guilty, and lost interest in things—or is this the first time you’ve experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve had times before where I was stressed or unhappy, like anyone has, but I don’t know if it was the same as this. I haven’t really thought of my life in terms of “episodes,” if that makes sense.\n\nThere have been periods where I was more withdrawn or harder on myself, but I can’t confidently say, yes, this exact thing happened before. I don’t want to exaggerate or make it sound like there’s a clear pattern if I’m not sure there is one. This feels… noticeable to me now, but whether it’s the first time or just the first time I’m paying this much attention to it, I honestly don’t know.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back and recall if there were any distinct times in the past when you felt down, hopeless, or lost interest in things for a period of at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of it in the way you’re asking—like a distinct block of time, two weeks or more, where I could say, yes, I was clearly down or hopeless or not interested in things. And honestly, I don’t know that I can identify one clearly.\n\nI’ve had rough stretches before, sure. There were times I was overwhelmed or not really myself, times I probably pulled back from people or went through the motions more than anything. But when I look back, it all kind of blends into life stress, work stress, being tired, dealing with responsibilities… I don’t have a clean memory of, “this started here and lasted until there,” if that makes sense.\n\nPart of me worries I’m just not remembering it right. I tend to downplay things after the fact, or tell myself it wasn’t that bad. But I also don’t want to make something sound more definite than it is. So the most honest answer is: I can’t really recall a distinct past period like that with certainty. I know I’ve had difficult times, but I can’t say confidently that there was a clear two-week episode before.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m still having trouble pinning down a past time like that, honestly. When you say “at least two weeks in a row,” I start trying to sort through things and I can’t quite separate what was a real stretch of being down from what was just stress or being worn out.

What I can say is that lately, most days I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling. It’s like… I don’t know how else to put it, like the color has gone out of things. I can get myself to my desk in HR and sit there looking at emails or forms or whatever is in front of me, and it all feels very far away, like it doesn’t really reach me. And even at home, I’ll look out at the garden beds—things I used to notice, or care about—and I just feel kind of blank and low.

So if you’re asking whether I recognize that feeling, yes, I do now. But whether there was a clear time in the past where it lasted two weeks or more, I’m not sure I can honestly say. Right now it feels like it’s most days, and it’s hard to remember if it was ever this defined before.

I think… I’m still not sure about “ever,” if I’m being really accurate. I don’t want to say yes to something from the past if I can’t actually picture it clearly. But what I can say is that what’s happening now feels like that kind of real change.

Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I… I came in because I can’t seem to get past this feeling—no, it’s more than a feeling, it feels like I know it—that I’m being punished for what happened with my father. For letting him decline. I keep going over it in my head, all the decisions, all the appointments, the things I did and didn’t notice, and it feels like there’s already been some kind of judgment made about me.\n\nMy sister keeps trying to show me the doctor’s notes, and she tells me I did everything I was supposed to do, that I didn’t cause it, that no one thinks that. I know that probably sounds extreme, but I can’t shake it, and it’s been sitting on me constantly.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about this feeling of being punished—do you find that you blame yourself for things that weren’t really your fault, or do you feel guilty even when others say you did nothing wrong?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do. I know people keep telling me that it wasn’t all on me, that my father’s health was complicated and that I couldn’t control everything, but I still go back over every single decision like I’m building a case against myself.\n\nLike, I think about his appointments—whether I scheduled them quickly enough, whether I asked the right questions, whether I should have pushed harder or noticed something sooner. And then I think about conversations with my relatives, especially the tense ones, and I replay every word I said. If I got defensive, or if I sounded tired, or if I didn’t answer someone right away, it turns into proof in my head that I’m selfish. Like I was more concerned with my own stress than with being a good daughter.\n\nAnd it doesn’t stay only with my father, either. It spreads. I’ll be at work and make some small mistake or take too long to respond to something, and suddenly it feels like I’m not just struggling, I’m useless. Even when nothing is actively going wrong, it’s like my mind finds a way back to it. I keep going over every decision I made about my father’s appointments—whether I called soon enough, whether I should’ve asked more, whether I missed something obvious—and then it turns into this conclusion that I failed him.\n\nAnd it’s not just that. I replay those tense conversations with relatives, too. If I was short with someone, or if I seemed frustrated, I end up thinking, see, that’s who you are, you’re selfish, you made it about yourself. It gets very… fixed in my head.\n\nThen at work, if I make even a small mistake or don’t handle something perfectly, I start thinking I’m useless there too. Like I’m a selfish daughter and a useless employee, and whatever happens to me after that is something I’ve earned somehow. Even when things are technically okay, I don’t really feel okay inside.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself losing interest in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies, work, or spending time with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not sure how to answer that clearly. I don’t know if I’d say I’ve noticed it in a clean way, like “I used to love this and now I don’t.” It’s more muddled than that.\n\nI still do some things, or at least I think about doing them, but I can’t always tell whether I’m not interested or whether I’m just too caught up in everything else in my head. Like with friends or family, I sometimes don’t know if I’m avoiding people because I don’t enjoy being around them, or because I feel uncomfortable and don’t know how to act normal. And work is… work. I go through it, but I wouldn’t really describe it as enjoyment exactly.\n\nSo I guess I haven’t sat down and thought, “I’ve lost interest in all my hobbies.” I don’t even know what counts right now. It’s just that everything feels harder to sort out than it used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for the past couple of weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. Most days, when I wake up, there’s this heavy feeling already there, before anything has even happened. It’s hard to describe without sounding dramatic, but it feels hollow, like the color has gone out of everything.\n\nI’ll get to work and sit at my desk in HR, looking at emails or forms or whatever is in front of me, and it’s like I’m there physically but nothing really reaches me. Or I’ll look out at the garden beds at home—something I used to at least notice, the plants, the little changes—and I just feel… flat. Not peaceful, not rested, just kind of emptied out.\n\nIt’s not every single second, I suppose, but it’s there most of the day, most days. Like a weight I’m carrying around even when I’m trying to act normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your energy level or felt more fatigued than usual, even when you haven’t been doing much physically?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I feel tired a lot, but I’m not sure if it’s a separate thing or just… everything else wearing me down.\n\nSome days I come home from work and I feel like I’ve done much more than I actually did, if that makes sense. Like even sitting through normal tasks or answering emails can feel like it took a lot out of me. But then I wonder if that counts as fatigue, or if I’m just mentally drained.\n\nI haven’t been doing anything especially physical, no. I’m not out exercising or lifting things or anything like that. It’s more like I don’t feel refreshed very often, but I don’t know how to measure it. I wouldn’t want to say it wrong. I’ve noticed I feel worn down, but I’m not sure whether my actual energy level has changed or whether I’m just noticing it more because everything feels harder.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently—like eating more or less than usual, or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking my weight or anything, so I don’t want to say I’ve lost or gained if I don’t actually know. My clothes seem… I don’t know, about the same, I think.\n\nWith eating, it’s a little hard for me to answer clearly. Some days I eat because it’s time to eat, not because I particularly want anything. Other times I’ll realize I haven’t paid much attention to whether I’m hungry or not. But I can’t say there’s been some obvious big change, like suddenly eating twice as much or barely eating at all.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve noticed a clear appetite or weight change. It’s more that I’m not very tuned in to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep recently—like having trouble falling asleep, waking up during the night, or waking up earlier than you want to?\n\nPatient: Yes. Sleep has definitely been… off. I don’t usually fall asleep when I mean to anymore. I’ll get into bed and I’m exhausted, but then my mind starts going over phone calls from the day, or decisions I made about someone’s care, or whether I said the wrong thing or missed something, and it just loops. I’ll replay the same conversation in my head like I can somehow fix it by thinking about it harder.\n\nSo I end up falling asleep late, later than I should, even when I’m lying there trying not to think. And then I’ll wake up around 3 or 4 in the morning, almost like my body has decided that’s it, sleep is over. My chest feels tight when I wake up, and I’ll lie there staring at the dark, trying to get comfortable or calm down, but I usually can’t really get back to sleep properly before I have to get up for work.\n\nIt’s frustrating because by the time morning comes, I feel like I never really had a full night. I’m not staying up doing anything useful, either. It’s just… lying there, thinking and waking too early.\n\nDoctor: Have you had episodes like this before—where you felt down, guilty, and lost interest in things—or is this the first time you’ve experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that. There were times I was overwhelmed or not really myself, times I probably pulled back from people or went through the motions more than anything. Before this current period… I still don’t think I can clearly identify one. I know you’re asking very specifically about before now, and I’ve been trying to scan back through different stressful times, but I can’t honestly say, “yes, there was a definite two-week stretch where I felt that way nearly every day.”

What I can describe clearly is now. Most days I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling, like something has drained the color out of everything before the day even starts. I’ll get myself to work and sit at my desk in HR, and it’s like I’m looking at emails or forms or whatever I’m supposed to be doing, but none of it really reaches me. I’m there, technically, but I feel very far away from it.

And at home, I’ll look out at the garden beds, which used to at least catch my attention, and I just sort of stare. I don’t feel connected to it. It’s like I’m watching my own life from a distance, and everything feels blank and low.

So before this, I’m not sure I can give you a solid example. But this current feeling, yes, this is very noticeable and it feels different from just being stressed.

    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.3, F33.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I… I came in because I can’t seem to get past this feeling—no, it’s more than a feeling, it feels like I know it—that I’m being punished for what happened with my father. For letting him decline. I keep going over it in my head, all the decisions, all the appointments, the things I did and didn’t notice, and it feels like there’s already been some kind of judgment made about me.\n\nMy sister keeps trying to show me the doctor’s notes, and she tells me I did everything I was supposed to do, that I didn’t cause it, that no one thinks that. And I can understand the words when she says them, but they don’t… they don’t land. It still feels certain, like I failed him and now this is what I deserve somehow. I know that probably sounds extreme, but I can’t shake it, and it’s been sitting on me constantly.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about this feeling of being punished—do you find that you blame yourself for things that weren’t really your fault, or do you feel guilty even when others say you did nothing wrong?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do. I know people keep telling me that it wasn’t all on me, that my father’s health was complicated and that I couldn’t control everything, but I still go back over every single decision like I’m building a case against myself.\n\nLike, I think about his appointments—whether I scheduled them quickly enough, whether I asked the right questions, whether I should have pushed harder or noticed something sooner. And then I think about conversations with my relatives, especially the tense ones, and I replay every word I said. If I got defensive, or if I sounded tired, or if I didn’t answer someone right away, it turns into proof in my head that I’m selfish. Like I was more concerned with my own stress than with being a good daughter.\n\nAnd it doesn’t stay only with my father, either. It spreads. I’ll be at work and make some small mistake or take too long to respond to something, and suddenly it feels like I’m not just struggling, I’m useless. A useless employee, a selfish daughter… those are the words that keep coming up. And then there’s this awful part of me that thinks, well, maybe whatever happens to me now is deserved. Like I don’t really get to complain because I brought it on myself somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this sense of worthlessness or that you are a bad or selfish person most days, even when things are going okay?\n\nPatient: Yes… most days, yes. Even when nothing is actively going wrong, it’s like my mind finds a way back to it. I keep going over every decision I made about my father’s appointments—whether I called soon enough, whether I should’ve asked more, whether I missed something obvious—and then it turns into this conclusion that I failed him.\n\nAnd it’s not just that. I replay those tense conversations with relatives, too. If I was short with someone, or if I seemed frustrated, I end up thinking, see, that’s who you are, you’re selfish, you made it about yourself. It gets very… fixed in my head.\n\nThen at work, if I make even a small mistake or don’t handle something perfectly, I start thinking I’m useless there too. Like I’m a selfish daughter and a useless employee, and whatever happens to me after that is something I’ve earned somehow. Even when things are technically okay, I don’t really feel okay inside.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself losing interest in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies, work, or spending time with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not sure how to answer that clearly. I don’t know if I’d say I’ve noticed it in a clean way, like “I used to love this and now I don’t.” It’s more muddled than that.\n\nI still do some things, or at least I think about doing them, but I can’t always tell whether I’m not interested or whether I’m just too caught up in everything else in my head. Like with friends or family, I sometimes don’t know if I’m avoiding people because I don’t enjoy being around them, or because I feel uncomfortable and don’t know how to act normal. And work is… work. I go through it, but I wouldn’t really describe it as enjoyment exactly.\n\nSo I guess I haven’t sat down and thought, “I’ve lost interest in all my hobbies.” I don’t even know what counts right now. It’s just that everything feels harder to sort out than it used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for the past couple of weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. Most days, when I wake up, there’s this heavy feeling already there, before anything has even happened. It’s hard to describe without sounding dramatic, but it feels hollow, like the color has gone out of everything.\n\nI’ll get to work and sit at my desk in HR, looking at emails or forms or whatever is in front of me, and it’s like I’m there physically but nothing really reaches me. Or I’ll look out at the garden beds at home—something I used to at least notice, the plants, the little changes—and I just feel… flat. Not peaceful, not rested, just kind of emptied out.\n\nIt’s not every single second, I suppose, but it’s there most of the day, most days. Like a weight I’m carrying around even when I’m trying to act normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your energy level or felt more fatigued than usual, even when you haven’t been doing much physically?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I feel tired a lot, but I’m not sure if it’s a separate thing or just… everything else wearing me down.\n\nSome days I come home from work and I feel like I’ve done much more than I actually did, if that makes sense. Like even sitting through normal tasks or answering emails can feel like it took a lot out of me. But then I wonder if that counts as fatigue, or if I’m just mentally drained.\n\nI haven’t been doing anything especially physical, no. I’m not out exercising or lifting things or anything like that. It’s more like I don’t feel refreshed very often, but I don’t know how to measure it. I wouldn’t want to say it wrong. I’ve noticed I feel worn down, but I’m not sure whether my actual energy level has changed or whether I’m just noticing it more because everything feels harder.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently—like eating more or less than usual, or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking my weight or anything, so I don’t want to say I’ve lost or gained if I don’t actually know. My clothes seem… I don’t know, about the same, I think.\n\nWith eating, it’s a little hard for me to answer clearly. Some days I eat because it’s time to eat, not because I particularly want anything. Other times I’ll realize I haven’t paid much attention to whether I’m hungry or not. But I can’t say there’s been some obvious big change, like suddenly eating twice as much or barely eating at all.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve noticed a clear appetite or weight change. It’s more that I’m not very tuned in to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep recently—like having trouble falling asleep, waking up during the night, or waking up earlier than you want to?\n\nPatient: Yes. Sleep has definitely been… off. I don’t usually fall asleep when I mean to anymore. I’ll get into bed and I’m exhausted, but then my mind starts going over phone calls from the day, or decisions I made about someone’s care, or whether I said the wrong thing or missed something, and it just loops. I’ll replay the same conversation in my head like I can somehow fix it by thinking about it harder.\n\nSo I end up falling asleep late, later than I should, even when I’m lying there trying not to think. And then I’ll wake up around 3 or 4 in the morning, almost like my body has decided that’s it, sleep is over. My chest feels tight when I wake up, and I’ll lie there staring at the dark, trying to get comfortable or calm down, but I usually can’t really get back to sleep properly before I have to get up for work.\n\nIt’s frustrating because by the time morning comes, I feel like I never really had a full night. I’m not staying up doing anything useful, either. It’s just… lying there, thinking and waking too early.\n\nDoctor: Have you had episodes like this before—where you felt down, guilty, and lost interest in things—or is this the first time you’ve experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve had times before where I was stressed or unhappy, like anyone has, but I don’t know if it was the same as this. I haven’t really thought of my life in terms of “episodes,” if that makes sense.\n\nThere have been periods where I was more withdrawn or harder on myself, but I can’t confidently say, yes, this exact thing happened before. I don’t want to exaggerate or make it sound like there’s a clear pattern if I’m not sure there is one. This feels… noticeable to me now, but whether it’s the first time or just the first time I’m paying this much attention to it, I honestly don’t know.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back and recall if there were any distinct times in the past when you felt down, hopeless, or lost interest in things for a period of at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of it in the way you’re asking—like a distinct block of time, two weeks or more, where I could say, yes, I was clearly down or hopeless or not interested in things. And honestly, I don’t know that I can identify one clearly.\n\nI’ve had rough stretches before, sure. There were times I was overwhelmed or not really myself, times I probably pulled back from people or went through the motions more than anything. But when I look back, it all kind of blends into life stress, work stress, being tired, dealing with responsibilities… I don’t have a clean memory of, “this started here and lasted until there,” if that makes sense.\n\nPart of me worries I’m just not remembering it right. I tend to downplay things after the fact, or tell myself it wasn’t that bad. But I also don’t want to make something sound more definite than it is. So the most honest answer is: I can’t really recall a distinct past period like that with certainty. I know I’ve had difficult times, but I can’t say confidently that there was a clear two-week episode before.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall a time in the past when you felt down or hopeless for at least two weeks in a row, even if it was during a stressful time in your life?\n\nPatient: I’m still having trouble pinning down a past time like that, honestly. When you say “at least two weeks in a row,” I start trying to sort through things and I can’t quite separate what was a real stretch of being down from what was just stress or being worn out.\n\nWhat I can say is that lately, most days I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling. It’s like… I don’t know how else to put it, like the color has gone out of things. I can get myself to my desk in HR and sit there looking at emails or forms or whatever is in front of me, and it all feels very far away, like it doesn’t really reach me. And even at home, I’ll look out at the garden beds—things I used to notice, or care about—and I just feel kind of blank and low.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether I recognize that feeling, yes, I do now. But whether there was a clear time in the past where it lasted two weeks or more, I’m not sure I can honestly say. Right now it feels like it’s most days, and it’s hard to remember if it was ever this defined before.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life when you felt down or hopeless nearly every day for two weeks or more, to the point where you noticed a real change in how you were feeling and functioning, even if it was during a stressful time?\n\nPatient: I think… I’m still not sure about “ever,” if I’m being really accurate. I don’t want to say yes to something from the past if I can’t actually picture it clearly. But what I can say is that what’s happening now feels like that kind of real change.\n\nMost days now, I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling, like something is already wrong before anything has even happened. It’s not just being tired or having a bad morning. It feels like the color has gone out of everything. I can go to work and sit at my desk in HR, looking at emails or forms or whatever I’m supposed to be doing, and it’s like none of it really reaches me. I’m there, but I don’t feel connected to it.\n\nAnd at home it’s similar. I’ll look out at the garden beds, and normally I’d notice what needed watering or what was growing, or at least feel something about it, but now I just kind of stare at them and feel blank and low. So yes, in terms of noticing a change in how I’m feeling and functioning, I do notice that now. I just can’t honestly say whether there was a past period like this for two weeks or more.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to any time before this current period when you felt down or hopeless nearly every day for at least two weeks, where it affected how you felt or functioned, even if it was during a stressful time?\n\nPatient: Before this current period… I still don’t think I can clearly identify one. Before my father’s decline… I’m trying to be careful with that, because I don’t want to invent a memory just to make it fit. I can’t really pull up a clear period before then where I can say, yes, it was nearly every day for two weeks and it changed how I functioned.

Even when nothing is actively going wrong, it’s like my mind finds a way back to it. Like I’m a selfish daughter and a useless employee, and whatever happens to me after that is something I’ve earned somehow. I haven’t been tracking my weight or anything, so I don’t want to say I’ve lost or gained if I don’t actually know. My clothes seem… I don’t know, about the same, I think.\n\nWith eating, it’s a little hard for me to answer clearly. Some days I eat because it’s time to eat, not because I particularly want anything. Other times I’ll realize I haven’t paid much attention to whether I’m hungry or not. Sleep has definitely been… off. I don’t usually fall asleep when I mean to anymore. I’ll get into bed and I’m exhausted, but then my mind starts going over phone calls from the day, or decisions I made about someone’s care, or whether I said the wrong thing or missed something, and it just loops. I’ll replay the same conversation in my head like I can somehow fix it by thinking about it harder.\n\nSo I end up falling asleep late, later than I should, even when I’m lying there trying not to think. And then I’ll wake up around 3 or 4 in the morning, almost like my body has decided that’s it, sleep is over. I’m not staying up doing anything useful, either. It’s just… lying there, thinking and waking too early.\n\nDoctor: Have you had episodes like this before—where you felt down, guilty, and lost interest in things—or is this the first time you’ve experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that. I haven’t really thought of my life in terms of “episodes,” if that makes sense.\n\nThere have been periods where I was more withdrawn or harder on myself, but I can’t confidently say, yes, this exact thing happened before. I don’t want to exaggerate or make it sound like there’s a clear pattern if I’m not sure there is one. This feels… noticeable to me now, but whether it’s the first time or just the first time I’m paying this much attention to it, I honestly don’t know.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back and recall if there were any distinct times in the past when you felt down, hopeless, or lost interest in things for a period of at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of it in the way you’re asking—like a distinct block of time, two weeks or more, where I could say, yes, I was clearly down or hopeless or not interested in things. When you say “at least two weeks in a row,” I start trying to sort through things and I can’t quite separate what was a real stretch of being down from what was just stress or being worn out.\n\nWhat I can say is that lately, most days I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling. It’s like… I don’t know how else to put it, like the color has gone out of things. I can get myself to my desk in HR and sit there looking at emails or forms or whatever is in front of me, and it all feels very far away, like it doesn’t really reach me. And even at home, I’ll look out at the garden beds—things I used to notice, or care about—and I just feel kind of blank and low.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether I recognize that feeling, yes, I do now. But whether there was a clear time in the past where it lasted two weeks or more, I’m not sure I can honestly say. Right now it feels like it’s most days, and it’s hard to remember if it was ever this defined before.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life when you felt down or hopeless nearly every day for two weeks or more, to the point where you noticed a real change in how you were feeling and functioning, even if it was during a stressful time?\n\nPatient: I think… I’m still not sure about “ever,” if I’m being really accurate. I don’t want to say yes to something from the past if I can’t actually picture it clearly. But what I can say is that what’s happening now feels like that kind of real change.\n\nMost days now, I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling, like something is already wrong before anything has even happened. It’s not just being tired or having a bad morning. It feels like the color has gone out of everything. I can go to work and sit at my desk in HR, looking at emails or forms or whatever I’m supposed to be doing, and it’s like none of it really reaches me. I’m there, but I don’t feel connected to it.\n\nAnd at home it’s similar. I’ll look out at the garden beds, and normally I’d notice what needed watering or what was growing, or at least feel something about it, but now I just kind of stare at them and feel blank and low. I just can’t honestly say whether there was a past period like this for two weeks or more.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to any time before this current period when you felt down or hopeless nearly every day for at least two weeks, where it affected how you felt or functioned, even if it was during a stressful time?\n\nPatient: Before this current period… I still don’t think I can clearly identify one. Most days I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling, like something has drained the color out of everything before the day even starts. Before my father became ill… I’m trying to think about that carefully, because I don’t want to just say yes because it sounds like what you’re asking for. I honestly can’t pull up a clear earlier stretch like that, not in a way I’d feel confident about.

Like there’s a pane of glass between me and whatever I’m supposed to care about or respond to. For letting him decline. I keep going over it in my head, all the decisions, all the appointments, the things I did and didn’t notice, and it feels like there’s already been some kind of judgment made about me.\n\nMy sister keeps trying to show me the doctor’s notes, and she tells me I did everything I was supposed to do, that I didn’t cause it, that no one thinks that. And I can understand the words when she says them, but they don’t… they don’t land. It still feels certain, like I failed him and now this is what I deserve somehow. I know that probably sounds extreme, but I can’t shake it, and it’s been sitting on me constantly.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about this feeling of being punished—do you find that you blame yourself for things that weren’t really your fault, or do you feel guilty even when others say you did nothing wrong?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do. I know people keep telling me that it wasn’t all on me, that my father’s health was complicated and that I couldn’t control everything, but I still go back over every single decision like I’m building a case against myself.\n\nLike, I think about his appointments—whether I scheduled them quickly enough, whether I asked the right questions, whether I should have pushed harder or noticed something sooner. And then I think about conversations with my relatives, especially the tense ones, and I replay every word I said. If I got defensive, or if I sounded tired, or if I didn’t answer someone right away, it turns into proof in my head that I’m selfish. Like I was more concerned with my own stress than with being a good daughter.\n\nAnd it doesn’t stay only with my father, either. It spreads. I’ll be at work and make some small mistake or take too long to respond to something, and suddenly it feels like I’m not just struggling, I’m useless. A useless employee, a selfish daughter… those are the words that keep coming up. And then there’s this awful part of me that thinks, well, maybe whatever happens to me now is deserved. Like I don’t really get to complain because I brought it on myself somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this sense of worthlessness or that you are a bad or selfish person most days, even when things are going okay?\n\nPatient: Yes… most days, yes. Even when nothing is actively going wrong, it’s like my mind finds a way back to it. I keep going over every decision I made about my father’s appointments—whether I called soon enough, whether I should’ve asked more, whether I missed something obvious—and then it turns into this conclusion that I failed him.\n\nAnd it’s not just that. I replay those tense conversations with relatives, too. If I was short with someone, or if I seemed frustrated, I end up thinking, see, that’s who you are, you’re selfish, you made it about yourself. Like I’m a selfish daughter and a useless employee, and whatever happens to me after that is something I’ve earned somehow. Like with friends or family, I sometimes don’t know if I’m avoiding people because I don’t enjoy being around them, or because I feel uncomfortable and don’t know how to act normal. I go through it, but I wouldn’t really describe it as enjoyment exactly.\n\nSo I guess I haven’t sat down and thought, “I’ve lost interest in all my hobbies.” I don’t even know what counts right now. It’s just that everything feels harder to sort out than it used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for the past couple of weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. Most days, when I wake up, there’s this heavy feeling already there, before anything has even happened. Not peaceful, not rested, just kind of emptied out.\n\nIt’s not every single second, I suppose, but it’s there most of the day, most days. Like a weight I’m carrying around even when I’m trying to act normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your energy level or felt more fatigued than usual, even when you haven’t been doing much physically?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I feel tired a lot, but I’m not sure if it’s a separate thing or just… everything else wearing me down.\n\nSome days I come home from work and I feel like I’ve done much more than I actually did, if that makes sense. Like even sitting through normal tasks or answering emails can feel like it took a lot out of me. But then I wonder if that counts as fatigue, or if I’m just mentally drained.\n\nI haven’t been doing anything especially physical, no. I’m not out exercising or lifting things or anything like that. It’s more like I don’t feel refreshed very often, but I don’t know how to measure it. I wouldn’t want to say it wrong. I’ve noticed I feel worn down, but I’m not sure whether my actual energy level has changed or whether I’m just noticing it more because everything feels harder.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently—like eating more or less than usual, or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking my weight or anything, so I don’t want to say I’ve lost or gained if I don’t actually know. My clothes seem… I don’t know, about the same, I think.\n\nWith eating, it’s a little hard for me to answer clearly. Some days I eat because it’s time to eat, not because I particularly want anything. Other times I’ll realize I haven’t paid much attention to whether I’m hungry or not. But I can’t say there’s been some obvious big change, like suddenly eating twice as much or barely eating at all.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve noticed a clear appetite or weight change. It’s more that I’m not very tuned in to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep recently—like having trouble falling asleep, waking up during the night, or waking up earlier than you want to?\n\nPatient: Yes. I’ll replay the same conversation in my head like I can somehow fix it by thinking about it harder.\n\nSo I end up falling asleep late, later than I should, even when I’m lying there trying not to think. I tend to downplay things after the fact, or tell myself it wasn’t that bad. But I also don’t want to make something sound more definite than it is.
